╭─ ⚕ Hermes ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
    The Madelung bridge connects directly to the phase axis K = J·ε:


    PolarizedMadelungBridge:
      PolarizedDoubledAmplitude ψ   (‖ψ‖ = 1, on doubled Krein space)
        → ψplus, ψminus             (spectral sheet decomposition)
        → positiveDensity, negativeDensity
        → phaseOrbit θ              (= exp(θ·K) · ψ, U(1) phase rotation)

    BohmMadelungOperatorialBridge:
      StateGeneratorField
        → statePhaseReadout          (= metric·K, phase via complex structure)
        → phaseLinearPart            (K-linear: [A,K] = 0)
        → phaseAntilinearPart        (K-antilinear: A·K = -K·A)
        → modularConjugationJ        (J on phase orbit reverses direction)

    HestenesRealStructures:
  💾 Self-improvement review: User profile updated
      KLinear / KAntilinear operators
      MajoranaRealStructure (real involution, fixed sector)


    The chain is:

    K = J·ε (phase axis, K² = -1)
      ↓
    phaseOrbit under K → U(1) phase rotations of the Madelung amplitude
      ↓
    statePhaseReadout = contraction of metric with K
      ↓
    tilt (chirality, from ChiralAnomalyCantor)
      + K (complex structure)
      + J (real structure, J·K = -K·J)
      → real spectral triple KO-commutation relations


    The Madelung phase orbit exp(θ·K) is exactly the U(1) gauge symmetry whose anomaly cancellation is proved in ChiralAnomalyCantor.chiral_anomaly_vanishes_at_flat_boundary: the index Tr(tilt·e) measures the difference between left and right phase orbits, and it vanishes when D·e = e·D (the flat KMS boundary condition).

$         cd /home/goutev/repos/info-geometry-lean && grep -rn 'fermion.*operator\|fermionic.*hamiltonian\|boson.*fermion.*swap\|role.*swap\|change.*role\|fermion.*spectrum\|boson.*spectrum' lean/InfoGeometry/Arithmetic/ --include='*.lean' | head -10  0.1s

╭─ ⚕ Hermes ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
    The swap: the bosonic partition Tr(e^{-sH}) = ζ(s) has a pole at s=1. The fermionic partition Tr(Γ·e^{-sH}) = 1/ζ(s) has poles at the zeros of ζ(s).

    lean
    Bosonic:  Tr_{Sym}(e^{-sH})      = ζ(s)      pole at s=1
    Fermionic: Tr_{∧}(Γ·e^{-sH})    = 1/ζ(s)    poles at ζ(s)=0


    The operator whose spectral sum gives the Riemann zeros is the chiral-weighted heat kernel Γ·e^{-sH}:


    Γ·e^{-sH}|n⟩ = μ(n)·n^{-s}|n⟩


    Its eigenvalues are μ(n)·n^{-s}. The sum Σ μ(n)·n^{-s} converges for Re(s)>1 to 1/ζ(s). After analytic continuation, this sum has poles exactly where ζ(s)=0.

    The "fermionic Hamiltonian" is not diag(log n) but Γ·H — the Möbius-twisted energy operator. Its eigenvalues are μ(n)·log n (signed by the parity). The partition function Tr(e^{-s·Γ·H}) = Σ μ(n)·n^{-s} = 1/ζ(s). Its poles are the Riemann zeros.

    The Hilbert-Pólya operator in the repo is K = log(Γ·H) — the logarithm of the fermionic Hamiltonian. Its spectrum is the set of Riemann zeros─ ⚕ Hermes ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
    The honest state: the repo does not claim that K = log H has the Riemann zeros as eigenvalues. The connection is more subtle:


    det(1 - e^{-βH}) = 1/ζ(β)    [Fredholm = Möbius character, Re(β) > 1]

    Setting this to ZERO gives 1/ζ(β) = 0 → ζ(β) = ∞ → β = 1 (the POLE)


    The Fredholm determinant formula gives the pole of ζ, not the zeros. The zeros require analytic continuation to the critical strip, which is structural debt.

    What the repo DOES have:

    - HilbertPolyaOperatorPacket — conditional socket: "if you supply a self-adjoint operator and prove its spectrum equals the zeros, then RH holds"
    - dikinOmega_pos — the Dikin sandwich prevents zeros from leaving the critical line IF they are eigenvalues of a self-adjoint operator
    - criticalLine_zero_iff_spectral_value — the equivalence between zeros and spectral values, conditional on the Hilbert-Pólya hypothesis

    The gap: constructing a concrete self-adjoint operator K on ℓ²(ℕ^+) whose eigenvalues are the imaginary parts of the Riemann zeros, and proving ξ(1/2 + iK) = 0. This is not proved in the repo. It is the open Hilbert-Pólya conjecture — documented as a conditional socket, not a theorem.
